{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01CghHft2GZrrCmoY4sm49Xp","type":"tool_result","content":"{\"markdown\":\"---\\ntitle: How does the HDA Intel sound driver handle suspend to RAM (PM suspend) and resume? What code paths are involved in azx_get_response timeout handling and MSI disabling after resume? I'm looking for the suspend/resume implementation in hda_intel driver that might cause sound to stop working after multiple suspend cycles.\\nshare_url: https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/deepsearch/8b4a6087-999e-4bb5-9c8e-71e593d7ee7b\\ncreated: 2026-02-06T18:49:39Z\\nupdated: 2026-02-06T18:50:58Z\\n---\\n\\n# How does the HDA Intel sound driver handle suspend to RAM (PM suspend) and resume? What code paths are involved in azx_get_response timeout handling and MSI disabling after resume? I'm looking for the suspend/resume implementation in hda_intel driver that might cause sound to stop working after multiple suspend cycles.\\n\\nNow I have all the information needed. Let me provide a comprehensive answer:\\n\\n## HDA Intel Sound Driver Suspend/Resume Implementation\\n\\nThe HDA Intel driver's suspend/resume code paths are implemented in [sound/hda/controllers/intel.c](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/controllers/intel.c) and [sound/hda/common/controller.c](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/common/controller.c). Here's how they handle PM suspend and the timeout/MSI issues:\\n\\n### Suspend Path\\n\\nThe suspend flow ([azx_suspend](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/controllers/intel.c?L1038-L1051)):\\n\\n```c\\nstatic int azx_suspend(struct device *dev)\\n{\\n    struct snd_card *card = dev_get_drvdata(dev);\\n    struct azx *chip = card->private_data;\\n    \\n    azx_shutdown_chip(chip);\\n    return 0;\\n}\\n```\\n\\n[azx_shutdown_chip](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/controllers/intel.c?L897-L904) performs:\\n1. **azx_stop_chip** - Stops DMA and command I/O\\n2. **azx_enter_link_reset** - Puts controller in link reset\\n3. **azx_clear_irq_pending** - Clears pending interrupts\\n4. **display_power(false)** - Powers down display audio\\n\\n### Resume Path\\n\\nThe resume flow ([azx_resume](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/controllers/intel.c?L1053-L1067)) calls [__azx_runtime_resume](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/controllers/intel.c?L972-L1003):\\n\\n```c\\nstatic void __azx_runtime_resume(struct azx *chip)\\n{\\n    // 1. Read STATESTS before controller reset\\n    status = azx_readw(chip, STATESTS);\\n    \\n    // 2. Reinitialize PCI config (TCSEL, snoop, etc.)\\n    azx_init_pci(chip);\\n    \\n    // 3. Reset and initialize the controller\\n    hda_intel_init_chip(chip, true);\\n    \\n    // 4. Request codec resume for active codecs\\n    if (!chip->pm_prepared) {\\n        list_for_each_codec(codec, &chip->bus) {\\n            if (codec->forced_resume || (status & (1 << codec->addr)))\\n                pm_request_resume(hda_codec_dev(codec));\\n        }\\n    }\\n}\\n```\\n\\nKey initialization includes:\\n- [azx_init_pci](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/controllers/intel.c?L381-L439) - Restores PCI config (TCSEL, snoop settings)\\n- [hda_intel_init_chip](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/controllers/intel.c?L549-L570) - Resets controller with full_reset=true\\n\\n### azx_get_response Timeout Handling\\n\\nThe timeout handling in [azx_rirb_get_response](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/common/controller.c?L761-L824) has a **cascading fallback mechanism**:\\n\\n1. **First timeout → Polling mode** ([L776-L782](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/common/controller.c?L776-L782)):\\n   ```c\\n   if (!bus->polling_mode) {\\n       dev_warn(\\\"azx_get_response timeout, switching to polling mode\\\");\\n       bus->polling_mode = 1;\\n       goto again;  // Retry\\n   }\\n   ```\\n\\n2. **Second timeout with MSI → Disable MSI** ([L784-L792](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/common/controller.c?L784-L792)):\\n   ```c\\n   if (chip->msi) {\\n       dev_warn(\\\"No response from codec, disabling MSI\\\");\\n       if (chip->ops->disable_msi_reset_irq(chip) < 0)\\n           return -EIO;\\n       goto again;  // Retry\\n   }\\n   ```\\n\\n3. **Third timeout → Bus reset** ([L809-L814](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/common/controller.c?L809-L814)):\\n   ```c\\n   if (hbus->allow_bus_reset && !hbus->response_reset && !hbus->in_reset) {\\n       hbus->response_reset = 1;\\n       dev_err(\\\"No response from codec, resetting bus\\\");\\n       return -EAGAIN;  // Give codec a chance to retry\\n   }\\n   ```\\n\\n4. **Final fallback → Single command mode** ([L817-L823](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/common/controller.c?L817-L823))\\n\\n### MSI Disable Implementation\\n\\n[disable_msi_reset_irq](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/controllers/intel.c?L2053-L2068) completely tears down and re-establishes interrupts:\\n\\n```c\\nstatic int disable_msi_reset_irq(struct azx *chip)\\n{\\n    free_irq(bus->irq, chip);\\n    bus->irq = -1;\\n    pci_free_irq_vectors(chip->pci);\\n    chip->msi = 0;\\n    err = azx_acquire_irq(chip, 1);  // Re-acquire with legacy IRQ\\n    return err;\\n}\\n```\\n\\n### Potential Issues After Multiple Suspend Cycles\\n\\n**Root causes for sound failure after repeated suspend/resume:**\\n\\n1. **MSI state not restored properly** - If MSI gets disabled during a timeout ([L784-L792](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/common/controller.c?L784-L792)), it stays disabled permanently (`chip->msi = 0`). Subsequent resumes continue with legacy interrupts which may be unreliable on some systems.\\n\\n2. **Polling mode sticky** - Once `bus->polling_mode = 1` is set ([L780](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/common/controller.c?L780)), it's never cleared. The driver burns CPU polling instead of using interrupts.\\n\\n3. **Codec state desync** - The resume path reads `STATESTS` ([L984](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/controllers/intel.c?L984)) before controller reset. If codecs don't report presence correctly, they won't be resumed ([L995-L996](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/controllers/intel.c?L995-L996)).\\n\\n4. **PCI config race** - [azx_init_pci](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/controllers/intel.c?L381-L439) restores TCSEL and snoop settings, but if BIOS/firmware changes them during S3, timing-sensitive codec communication breaks.\\n\\n5. **Response timeout accumulation** - The `response_reset` flag ([L809-L821](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/common/controller.c?L809-L821)) triggers bus resets, but repeated failures can leave the controller in an inconsistent state.\\n\\n**Debugging recommendations:**\\n- Check `/sys/module/snd_hda_intel/parameters/enable_msi` - If MSI got disabled runtime\\n- Monitor dmesg for \\\"switching to polling mode\\\" or \\\"disabling MSI\\\" warnings\\n- Use `lspci -vv` to verify MSI state after resume\\n- Enable HDA codec tracing: `trace_azx_suspend` and `trace_azx_resume` ([L1049](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/controllers/intel.c?L1049), [L1065](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/controllers/intel.c?L1065))\\n\\n## Sources\\n\\n- How does the HDA Intel sound driver handle suspend to RAM (PM suspend) and resume? What code paths are involved in azx_get_response timeout handling and MSI disabling after resume? I'm looking for the suspend/resume implementation in hda_intel driver that might cause sound to stop working after multiple suspend cycles.\nshare_url: https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/deepsearch/8b4a6087-999e-4bb5-9c8e-71e593d7ee7b\ncreated: 2026-02-06T18:49:39Z\nupdated: 2026-02-06T18:50:58Z\n---\n\n# How does the HDA Intel sound driver handle suspend to RAM (PM suspend) and resume? What code paths are involved in azx_get_response timeout handling and MSI disabling after resume? I'm looking for the suspend/resume implementation in hda_intel driver that might cause sound to stop working after multiple suspend cycles.\n\nNow I have all the information needed. Let me provide a comprehensive answer:\n\n## HDA Intel Sound Driver Suspend/Resume Implementation\n\nThe HDA Intel driver's suspend/resume code paths are implemented in [sound/hda/controllers/intel.c](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/controllers/intel.c) and [sound/hda/common/controller.c](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/common/controller.c). Here's how they handle PM suspend and the timeout/MSI issues:\n\n### Suspend Path\n\nThe suspend flow ([azx_suspend](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/controllers/intel.c?L1038-L1051)):\n\n```c\nstatic int azx_suspend(struct device *dev)\n{\n    struct snd_card *card = dev_get_drvdata(dev);\n    struct azx *chip = card->private_data;\n    \n    azx_shutdown_chip(chip);\n    return 0;\n}\n```\n\n[azx_shutdown_chip](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/controllers/intel.c?L897-L904) performs:\n1. **azx_stop_chip** - Stops DMA and command I/O\n2. **azx_enter_link_reset** - Puts controller in link reset\n3. **azx_clear_irq_pending** - Clears pending interrupts\n4. **display_power(false)** - Powers down display audio\n\n### Resume Path\n\nThe resume flow ([azx_resume](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/controllers/intel.c?L1053-L1067)) calls [__azx_runtime_resume](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/controllers/intel.c?L972-L1003):\n\n```c\nstatic void __azx_runtime_resume(struct azx *chip)\n{\n    // 1. Read STATESTS before controller reset\n    status = azx_readw(chip, STATESTS);\n    \n    // 2. Reinitialize PCI config (TCSEL, snoop, etc.)\n    azx_init_pci(chip);\n    \n    // 3. Reset and initialize the controller\n    hda_intel_init_chip(chip, true);\n    \n    // 4. Request codec resume for active codecs\n    if (!chip->pm_prepared) {\n        list_for_each_codec(codec, &chip->bus) {\n            if (codec->forced_resume || (status & (1 << codec->addr)))\n                pm_request_resume(hda_codec_dev(codec));\n        }\n    }\n}\n```\n\nKey initialization includes:\n- [azx_init_pci](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/controllers/intel.c?L381-L439) - Restores PCI config (TCSEL, snoop settings)\n- [hda_intel_init_chip](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/controllers/intel.c?L549-L570) - Resets controller with full_reset=true\n\n### azx_get_response Timeout Handling\n\nThe timeout handling in [azx_rirb_get_response](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/common/controller.c?L761-L824) has a **cascading fallback mechanism**:\n\n1. **First timeout → Polling mode** ([L776-L782](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/common/controller.c?L776-L782)):\n   ```c\n   if (!bus->polling_mode) {\n       dev_warn(\"azx_get_response timeout, switching to polling mode\");\n       bus->polling_mode = 1;\n       goto again;  // Retry\n   }\n   ```\n\n2. **Second timeout with MSI → Disable MSI** ([L784-L792](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/common/controller.c?L784-L792)):\n   ```c\n   if (chip->msi) {\n       dev_warn(\"No response from codec, disabling MSI\");\n       if (chip->ops->disable_msi_reset_irq(chip) < 0)\n           return -EIO;\n       goto again;  // Retry\n   }\n   ```\n\n3. **Third timeout → Bus reset** ([L809-L814](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/common/controller.c?L809-L814)):\n   ```c\n   if (hbus->allow_bus_reset && !hbus->response_reset && !hbus->in_reset) {\n       hbus->response_reset = 1;\n       dev_err(\"No response from codec, resetting bus\");\n       return -EAGAIN;  // Give codec a chance to retry\n   }\n   ```\n\n4. **Final fallback → Single command mode** ([L817-L823](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/common/controller.c?L817-L823))\n\n### MSI Disable Implementation\n\n[disable_msi_reset_irq](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/controllers/intel.c?L2053-L2068) completely tears down and re-establishes interrupts:\n\n```c\nstatic int disable_msi_reset_irq(struct azx *chip)\n{\n    free_irq(bus->irq, chip);\n    bus->irq = -1;\n    pci_free_irq_vectors(chip->pci);\n    chip->msi = 0;\n    err = azx_acquire_irq(chip, 1);  // Re-acquire with legacy IRQ\n    return err;\n}\n```\n\n### Potential Issues After Multiple Suspend Cycles\n\n**Root causes for sound failure after repeated suspend/resume:**\n\n1. **MSI state not restored properly** - If MSI gets disabled during a timeout ([L784-L792](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/common/controller.c?L784-L792)), it stays disabled permanently (`chip->msi = 0`). Subsequent resumes continue with legacy interrupts which may be unreliable on some systems.\n\n2. **Polling mode sticky** - Once `bus->polling_mode = 1` is set ([L780](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/common/controller.c?L780)), it's never cleared. The driver burns CPU polling instead of using interrupts.\n\n3. **Codec state desync** - The resume path reads `STATESTS` ([L984](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/controllers/intel.c?L984)) before controller reset. If codecs don't report presence correctly, they won't be resumed ([L995-L996](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/controllers/intel.c?L995-L996)).\n\n4. **PCI config race** - [azx_init_pci](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/controllers/intel.c?L381-L439) restores TCSEL and snoop settings, but if BIOS/firmware changes them during S3, timing-sensitive codec communication breaks.\n\n5. **Response timeout accumulation** - The `response_reset` flag ([L809-L821](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/common/controller.c?L809-L821)) triggers bus resets, but repeated failures can leave the controller in an inconsistent state.\n\n**Debugging recommendations:**\n- Check `/sys/module/snd_hda_intel/parameters/enable_msi` - If MSI got disabled runtime\n- Monitor dmesg for \"switching to polling mode\" or \"disabling MSI\" warnings\n- Use `lspci -vv` to verify MSI state after resume\n- Enable HDA codec tracing: `trace_azx_suspend` and `trace_azx_resume` ([L1049](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/controllers/intel.c?L1049), [L1065](https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/r/github.com/torvalds/linux/-/blob/sound/hda/controllers/intel.c?L1065))\n\n## Sources\n\n- It\n    doesn't happen so often and usually it may read back successfully\n    after one or more seconds, but in some rare worst cases the driver\n    went into fallback mode.\n    \n    After trial-and-error, we found out that the communication stall seems\n    covered by issuing the sync after each verb write, as already done for\n    AMD and other chipsets. This state can be D3cold on platforms with ACPI PM support.\n    And runtime PM can provide a gerneral sysfs interface for a system policy\n    manager.\n    \n    Runtime PM support is based on current HDA power saving implementation. The user\n    can enable runtime PM on platfroms that provide acceptable latency on transition\n    from D3 to D0.\n    \n    Details:\n    - When both power saving and runtime PM are enabled:\n      -- If a codec supports 'stop-clock' in D3, it will request suspending the\n         controller after it enters D3 and request resuming the controller before\n         back to D0. Thus the controller will be suspended only when all codecs are\n         suspended and support stop-clock in D3.\n      -- User IO operations and HW wakeup signal can resume the controller back to\n         D0.\n    - If runtime PM is disabled, power saving just works as before.\n    - If power saving is disabled, the controller won't be suspended because the\n      power usage counter can never be 0.\n    \n    More about 'stop-clock' feature:\n    If a codec can support targeted pass-through operations in D3 state when there\n    is no BCLK present on the link, it will set CLKSTOP flag in the supported power\n    states and report PS-ClkStopOk when entering D3 state. Please refer to HDA spec\n    section 7.3.3.10 Power state and 7.3.4.12 Supported Power State.\n    \n    [Fixed CONFIG_PM_RUNTIME dependency in hda_intel.c by tiwai]\n    \n    Signed-off-by: This enabled runtime PM support, but this specific chipset has hardware issues with runtime PM that cause IRQ problems after suspend/resume cycles.\n\n**Symptoms**: After multiple suspend to RAM cycles, the controller fails to respond, triggering a cascade of fallback mechanisms:\n1. Timeout → Switch to polling mode (`azx_get_response timeout, switching to polling mode`)\n2. Continued timeout → Disable MSI (`No response from codec, disabling MSI`)\n3. Final fallback → Switch to single_cmd mode (`switching to single_cmd mode`)\n\n**Fix**: Change the driver_data from `AZX_DCAPS_INTEL_PCH` to `AZX_DCAPS_INTEL_PCH_NOPM`, which excludes runtime PM support for this specific device.
